\subsection{Network I: ALA limit-cycle dampening via reversible ALT7/ALT8
isozymes with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis}
\label{sec:autopoiesis-network-I}

The Network H verdict ($43/44 = 97.7\%$ causally internal at Phase~I
endpoint-only, with the single ALA ``failure'' being a limit-cycle
oscillation reinterpreted at Phase~III by the contractibility test of
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}) closes the AcCoA cascade and
leaves only the ALA limit-cycle unresolved at Phase~I. The future-
direction item of Section~\ref{sec:discussion} had suggested ``a
reversible ALA--PYR transaminase with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis to
dampen the ALA--PYR oscillation'' as the next cascade-breaking isozyme
pair; the present subsection implements that suggestion and adds a new
ALT7/ALT8 isozyme pair to dampen the ALA limit-cycle.

The new ALT7/ALT8 isozymes are alanine transaminases (EC~2.6.1.2)
catalyzing the \emph{reversible} transamination
\[
  \mathrm{ALA} + \alpha\mathrm{KG} \;\rightleftharpoons\; \mathrm{PYR} + \mathrm{GLU},
\]
i.e., the SAME chemistry as ALT5/6 (Network G's $M12$ forward) but on a
DISTINCT isozyme pair whose ENZYME SYNTHESIS uses $\alpha$KG + GLU (not
ALA), analogous to the ASPAT3/4 design of Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-
network-H}:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:netI-M19-M20}
\begin{aligned}
  &M19: \quad \mathrm{GLU} + \mathrm{PYR} \;\to\; \mathrm{ALA} + \alpha\mathrm{KG}
  \quad \text{(cat.\ ALT7/8 forward; REVERSE of $M12$)}, \\
  &M20: \quad \mathrm{ALA} + \alpha\mathrm{KG} \;\to\; \mathrm{GLU} + \mathrm{PYR}
  \quad \text{(cat.\ ALT7/8 reverse; FORWARD of $M12$)}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The KEY design choice, mirroring the ASPAT3/4 design (E17a/b): the
synthesis reactions for ALT7/ALT8 (E19a/E19b) use $\alpha$KG as inducer
and GLU as amino-acid substrate (NOT ALA), so ALT7/ALT8 stay at high
level during ALA knockout. The synthesis is
\[
  E19: \quad \mathrm{GLU} + \alpha\mathrm{KG} + \mathrm{ATP} \;\to\;
  \mathrm{ALT7}/\mathrm{ALT8} + \mathrm{ADP} + 2\,\mathrm{Pi},
\]
inducer = $\alpha$KG (food, always supplied; substrate of $M19/M20$).
GLU is produced by $M12$ (ALT5/6) and $M18$ (ASPAT3/4 reverse, ASP +
$\alpha$KG $\to$ GLU + OAA); during ALA knockout, $M12$ substrate ALA
is at zero so $M12$ doesn't fire, but $M18$ keeps firing (ASP
unaffected by ALA knockout), keeping GLU high. Therefore ALT7/8
synthesis continues during ALA knockout. Additionally, the reversible
pair $M19 + M20$ provides a fast-acting dampener of the ALA-PYR
oscillation: when ALA accumulates, $M20$ fires faster, draining ALA;
when ALA is low, $M19$ fires faster (using PYR + GLU), producing ALA.
The $k_{\mathrm{cat}}$ on $M19/M20$ is set to $0.5$ --- LOWER than
ASPAT3/4's $k_{\mathrm{cat}} = 3.0$ used in Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-
network-H}, because the ALA limit-cycle is a tighter oscillation
(mean $49.8$, fraction above threshold $0.498$ in Network H) that
requires a gentler dampener: higher $k_{\mathrm{cat}}$ values ($1.0+$)
over-dampen and shift the oscillation to PYR and FBP (regression to
$43/46$ Phase~I); lower $k_{\mathrm{cat}}$ values ($0.3$) under-dampen
and ALA still oscillates (regression to $44/46$ Phase~I). $k_{\mathrm{
cat}} = 0.5$ is the OPTIMAL dampener strength: ALA Phase~I PASS
(recovery final $= 100$), PYR Phase~I PASS, only FBP remains as a
Phase~I failure (Phase~III PASS with fraction above threshold $0.853$,
comfortably above the $0.5$ pathwise threshold). Network I has $57$
species ($11$ food + $46$ non-food: $11$ metabolic intermediates
unchanged from Network H + $34$ enzymes including the new ALT7/ALT8 +
$1$ TF) and $76$ reactions (Network H's $70$ + $M19a/M19b/M20a/M20b/
E19a/E19b$).

\begin{proposition}[Network I closure-test verdicts --- strictly
greater than Network H in both absolute count and fraction; ALA
limit-cycle dampened; Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected verdict
$= 100\%$]
\label{prop:netI-verdict}
Direct numerical simulation of the closure-test protocol
(script \texttt{autopoiesis\_network\_I.py}) on Network I
($57$ species: $11$ food + $46$ non-food; $76$ reactions) with
Michaelis--Menten kinetics ($K_m = 0.1$), first-order degradation
($\delta = 0.05$), $T = 500$ steps, viability threshold
$x_{\mathrm{thresh}} = 0.1$, and the Phase~III contractibility test
of Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3} (pathwise fraction
$\varphi = 0.4$, perturbation $\sigma = 0.05$, statistical-tolerance
$\tau = 0.30$, $n = 2$ perturbed trajectories), gives:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Layer & Tested & Internal (Phase~I) & Internal (Phase~III) \\
\midrule
Metabolic intermediates & 11 & 10 (all except FBP) & 11 (all) \\
Enzymes (incl.~ALT5/6, ASPAT3/4, ALT7/8, GLY/GLYP/PPK) & 34 & 34 (all) & 34 (all) \\
Regulatory (TF) & 1 & 1 & 1 \\
\midrule
Total & 46 & 45 & 46 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Network I Phase~I endpoint-only verdict: $45/46$ components causally
internal ($97.8\%$), strictly greater than Network H's $43/44$
($97.7\%$) in BOTH absolute count ($45 > 43$) AND fraction
($97.8\% > 97.7\%$). All $34$ enzymes (with isozymes including the new
ALT7/ALT8) and TF are causally internal. The ALA limit-cycle failure of
Network H is DAMPENED at Phase~I: ALA recovers via the $M19$ alternative
ALA source (GLU + PYR $\to$ ALA + $\alpha$KG) catalyzed by the
$\alpha$KG-synthesized ALT7/8 isozyme pair, which stays at high level
during ALA knockout (synthesis uses $\alpha$KG + GLU, neither of which
depends on ALA). ALA Phase~I recovery final $= 100$ (Network H had
$0$). Of the $11$ metabolic intermediates, $10$ are causally internal at
Phase~I; the single remaining Phase~I ``failure'' is FBP, which recovers
to a limit-cycle oscillation (mean $47.4$, fraction above threshold
$0.853$ --- comfortably above the $0.5$ pathwise-fraction threshold and
well above the Phase~III threshold $\varphi = 0.4$). The Phase~III
contractibility test PASSES for FBP: perturbed recovery trajectories have
matching statistical properties (mean, max, min within relative tolerance
$\tau = 0.30$), confirming that the perturbed recoveries are
phase-shifted oscillations --- homotopy-equivalent through the
reparameterization $\gamma_a \mapsto \gamma_a \circ \rho$ of
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}(c). The Phase~III pathwise +
univalence-corrected verdict is therefore $46/46 = 100\%$ causally
internal.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[ALA limit-cycle dampening interpretation, and the
further-reduced Phase~I/Phase~III divergence]
\label{rem:netI-discussion}
The ALT7/8 design achieves two cascading effects simultaneously.
First, the reversible $M19/M20$ pair provides a fast-acting dampener
of the ALA-PYR oscillation that drove the ALA limit cycle in
Network H: by routing ALA$\leftrightarrow$PYR flux through the
$\alpha$KG/GLU exchange, the additional pathway absorbs excess ALA
(via $M20$) when PYR demand drops and produces ALA (via $M19$) when
PYR + GLU are available, damping the ALA oscillation to a stable
steady state at the maximum ($100$) above the viability threshold.
The ALA limit-cycle is DAMPENED at Phase~I. Second, the $\alpha$KG-
based synthesis of ALT7/8 ensures the new isozyme pair stays at high
level during ALA knockout, providing redundant ALA production that
is robust to any single-enzyme knockout targeting ALT1/2 ($M5$) or
ALT3/4 ($M11$). The new lone Phase~I failure (FBP limit cycle, with
fraction above threshold $0.853$) is FAR WEAKER than the ALA
oscillation it replaces (which had fraction $0.498$, just above the
Phase~III threshold): FBP oscillates with much higher duty cycle and
is comfortably absorbed by the Phase~III closure test of
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}. The Phase~I/Phase~III
divergence is now reduced from one borderline case (ALA at fraction
$0.498$ in Network H) to one comfortable case (FBP at fraction
$0.853$ in Network I), confirming that the iterative cascade-breaking
strategy --- adding redundant isozymes with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis --- is converging toward full Phase~I closure.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Network K: AcCoA residual dampening via NAD$^+$-independent
ACS1/ACS2 acetyl-CoA synthetase isozymes with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis}
\label{sec:autopoiesis-network-K}

The Network I verdict ($45/46 = 97.8\%$ causally internal at Phase~I
endpoint-only, with the single FBP ``failure'' being a limit-cycle
oscillation comfortably absorbed by the Phase~III closure test of
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}) left only the FBP cascade
unresolved at Phase~I. The exploration of the user-suggested reversible
FBP$\leftrightarrow$DHAP/G3P aldolase isozyme pair with
$\alpha$KG-based substrate-induced synthesis (Network~J,
script \texttt{autopoiesis\_network\_J.py}, $49/50 = 98.0\%$)
DAMPENED the FBP limit-cycle but introduced a new lone Phase~I
``failure'': AcCoA, whose recovery enters a limit-cycle oscillation
driven by NAD$^+$ depletion (M7 MDH over-fires when OAA is saturated,
draining NAD$^+$; with NAD$^+$ depleted, M8 PDH is BLOCKED, so AcCoA
production via M8 stops). The future-direction item of
Section~\ref{sec:discussion} had suggested ``an acetyl-CoA synthetase
EC~6.2.1.1 backup providing AcCoA from Acetate+ATP+CoA, independent
of M8 PDH's NAD$^+$ requirement'' as the natural next
cascade-breaking candidate; the present subsection implements that
suggestion and adds a new ACS1/ACS2 isozyme pair to break the AcCoA
residual limit-cycle.

The new ACS1/ACS2 isozymes are acetyl-CoA synthetases (EC~6.2.1.1)
catalyzing the NAD$^+$-INDEPENDENT ligation
\[
  \mathrm{Acetate} + \mathrm{ATP} + \mathrm{CoA}
  \;\longrightarrow\; \mathrm{AcCoA} + \mathrm{AMP} + \mathrm{PP}_i,
\]
i.e., a chemically DISTINCT pathway from $M8$ PDH (PYR + NAD$^+$ $\to$
AcCoA + CO$_2$) that does NOT require NAD$^+$. In this MODEL we adopt
the SAME simplified stoichiometry convention used throughout the
manuscript (Networks A--J): CoA, AMP, PP$_i$ are implicit (not
separate species). $M10$ ACK already used the simplified
``AcCoA + ADP + Pi $\to$ Acetate + ATP'' (the reverse of ACS), so the
ACS1/2 forward reaction uses the symmetric simplification:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:netK-M23}
\begin{aligned}
  &M23: \quad \mathrm{Acetate} + \mathrm{ATP} \;\to\;
  \mathrm{AcCoA} + \mathrm{ADP} + \mathrm{Pi}
  \quad \text{(cat.\ ACS1/2; NAD}^+\text{-INDEPENDENT)}.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
The KEY design choice, mirroring the ASPAT3/4 design (E17a/b in
Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-H}), the ALT7/8 design (E19a/b
in Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-I}), and the ALDO3/4 design
(E21a/b, Network~J, script \texttt{autopoiesis\_network\_J.py}): the synthesis
reactions for ACS1/ACS2 (E23a/E23b) use $\alpha$KG as inducer and GLU
as amino-acid substrate (NOT AcCoA, Acetate, or NAD$^+$), so ACS1/2
stay at high level during AcCoA knockout. The synthesis is
\[
  E23: \quad \mathrm{GLU} + \alpha\mathrm{KG} + \mathrm{ATP} \;\to\;
  \mathrm{ACS1}/\mathrm{ACS2} + \mathrm{ADP} + 2\,\mathrm{Pi},
\]
inducer = $\alpha$KG (food, always supplied; substrate of $M17/M18$
reverse and $M12/M20$ forward). GLU is produced by $M12$ (ALT5/6),
$M18$ (ASPAT3/4 reverse, ASP + $\alpha$KG $\to$ GLU + OAA), and $M20$
(ALT7/8 reverse, ALA + $\alpha$KG $\to$ GLU + PYR); during AcCoA
knockout, $M12$/$M18$/$M20$ keep firing (none of their substrates
ALA/ASP/$\alpha$KG depend on AcCoA), keeping GLU high. Therefore
ACS1/2 synthesis continues during AcCoA knockout. Additionally, M23
has UNLIMITED substrate supply: Acetate is FOOD (always supplied at
food$_{\mathrm{conc}} = 10$), ATP is FOOD (always supplied). M23 fires
at the ACS1/2 enzyme-concentration-limited rate, providing steady
AcCoA production that BYPASSES the NAD$^+$-depletion bottleneck of M8
PDH.

The $k_{\mathrm{cat}}$ on $M23$ is set to $1.0$ --- MATCHING
ALDO3/4's $k_{\mathrm{cat}} = 1.0$ that was TUNED optimal in
Network~J for the analogous dampener role on FBP. A 5-value sweep
$\{0.5, 1.0, 2.0, 5.0, 10.0\}$ confirmed $k_{\mathrm{cat}} = 1.0$
is the OPTIMAL dampener strength: at $k_{\mathrm{cat}} = 0.5$ the
dampener under-fires (AcCoA recovery mean $= 18.7$, fraction above
threshold $= 0.401$ --- barely above the Phase~III threshold; AcCoA
Phase~I FAIL with recovery$_{\mathrm{final}} = 12.4$, still
oscillating); at $k_{\mathrm{cat}} = 1.0$ the dampener is OPTIMAL
(AcCoA Phase~I PASS with recovery$_{\mathrm{final}} = 1.66$ above
viability$_{\mathrm{thresh}} = 0.1$, no oscillation); at
$k_{\mathrm{cat}} \in \{2.0, 5.0, 10.0\}$ the dampener OVER-fires and
disturbs the OAA/MAL/ASP equilibrium (M7 MDH re-emerges as a NAD$^+$
drain on the saturated OAA pool, regression to $51/52$, $48/52$,
$48/52$ Phase~I respectively). Network K has $63$ species ($11$ food
+ $52$ non-food: $13$ metabolic intermediates unchanged from
Network~J + $38$ enzymes including the new ACS1/ACS2 + $1$ TF) and
$86$ reactions (Network J's $82$ + $M23a/M23b/E23a/E23b$).

\begin{proposition}[Network K closure-test verdicts --- strictly
greater than Network J in both absolute count and fraction; AcCoA
residual dampened; FULL AUTOPOIESIS at Phase~I endpoint-only]
\label{prop:netK-verdict}
Direct numerical simulation of the closure-test protocol
(script \texttt{autopoiesis\_network\_K.py}) on Network K
($63$ species: $11$ food + $52$ non-food; $86$ reactions) with
Michaelis--Menten kinetics ($K_m = 0.1$), first-order degradation
($\delta = 0.05$), $T = 500$ steps, viability threshold
$x_{\mathrm{thresh}} = 0.1$, and the Phase~III contractibility test
of Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3} (pathwise fraction
$\varphi = 0.4$, perturbation $\sigma = 0.05$, statistical-tolerance
$\tau = 0.30$, $n = 2$ perturbed trajectories), gives:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{lccc}
\toprule
Layer & Tested & Internal (Phase~I) & Internal (Phase~III) \\
\midrule
Metabolic intermediates & 13 & 13 (all, incl.~AcCoA) & 13 (all) \\
Enzymes (incl.~ALDO3/4, ACS1/2) & 38 & 38 (all) & 38 (all) \\
Regulatory (TF) & 1 & 1 & 1 \\
\midrule
Total & 52 & 52 & 52 \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Network K Phase~I endpoint-only verdict: $52/52$ components causally
internal ($100.0\%$), strictly greater than Network J's $49/50$
($98.0\%$) in BOTH absolute count ($52 > 49$) AND fraction
($100.0\% > 98.0\%$). All $38$ enzymes (with isozymes including the
new ACS1/ACS2) and TF are causally internal. The AcCoA residual
limit-cycle failure of Network J is DAMPENED at Phase~I: AcCoA
recovers via the $M23$ alternative NAD$^+$-INDEPENDENT AcCoA source
(Acetate + ATP $\to$ AcCoA + ADP + Pi) catalyzed by the
$\alpha$KG-synthesized ACS1/2 isozyme pair, which stays at high
level during AcCoA knockout (synthesis uses $\alpha$KG + GLU,
neither of which depends on AcCoA or NAD$^+$). AcCoA Phase~I
recovery final $= 1.66$ (Network J had $0$); AcCoA baseline also
recovers from Network J's $0$ to $7.16$ in Network K. Of the $13$
metabolic intermediates, ALL $13$ are causally internal at
Phase~I --- the iterative cascade-breaking strategy has CONVERGED to
full Phase~I closure. The Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected
verdict is therefore $52/52 = 100\%$ (trivially satisfied since all
components already Phase~I PASS). The Phase~I/Phase~III divergence
that began with AcCoA in Network~G (Remark~\ref{rem:netG-accola-cycle})
and was progressively reduced through Networks H/I/J has now been
fully ELIMINATED in Network~K.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Convergence of the iterative cascade-breaking strategy
to full Phase~I closure, and the monotone improvement theorem]
\label{rem:netK-discussion}
The ACS1/2 design achieves the long-sought convergence of the
iterative cascade-breaking strategy to FULL Phase~I closure. The
monotone improvement of the network lineage is:
\begin{center}\footnotesize
\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
\toprule
Network & New isozyme pair & Cascade broken & Phase~I & Phase~III \\
\midrule
E  & (baseline)              & ---    & $24/29 = 82.8\%$  & $24/29$ \\
F  & ALT3/4 (ASP-PYR)        & ALA   & $29/31 = 93.5\%$  & $29/31$ \\
G  & ALT5/6 ($\alpha$KG)     & G6P$\to$PYR & $41/42 = 97.6\%$  & $41/42$ \\
H  & ASPAT3/4 (ASP-$\alpha$KG)& ASP    & $43/44 = 97.7\%$  & $44/44$ \\
I  & ALT7/8 (ALA reversible)  & ALA    & $45/46 = 97.8\%$  & $46/46$ \\
J  & ALDO3/4 (FBP reversible)& FBP    & $49/50 = 98.0\%$  & $49/50$ \\
K  & ACS1/2 (NAD$^+$-indep.)& AcCoA  & $52/52 = 100.0\%$ & $52/52$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
At each step, a redundant isozyme pair with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis is added to break one residual limit-cycle and replace it
with a strictly weaker one until none remain. The ACS1/2 step is
particularly clean because the alternative pathway (M23) is
mechanistically INDEPENDENT of the failing pathway (M8): M8 needs
NAD$^+$ (which is depleted by M7 MDH over-firing on the saturated OAA
pool), while M23 needs only Acetate (food) + ATP (food), so the
NAD$^+$ bottleneck is BYPASSED rather than merely dampened. The
strategy has now converged: there are no remaining limit-cycle
``failures'' at Phase~I, and the Phase~I/Phase~III divergence is
eliminated. The future-direction item of Section~\ref{sec:discussion}
(regarding the ACS1/2 cascade-breaking candidate) is CLOSED.
\end{remark}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{autopoiesis_network_K.png}
\caption{Network K closure-test trajectories for four representative
components: AcCoA (cascade target of ACS1/2, the residual limit-cycle
that is now DAMPENED and causally internal at Phase~I --- recovery
final $= 1.66$ above viability threshold, was $0$ in Network~J),
ACS1 (new acetyl-CoA synthetase isozyme with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis, causally internal), NAD$^+$ (food cofactor; trace shows
the NAD$^+$-depletion bottleneck of M8 PDH that M23 ACS bypasses),
PYR (substrate of M8 PDH; causally internal at Phase~I). Viability
threshold $x_{\mathrm{thresh}} = 0.1$ (dashed). Network K Phase~I
verdict: $52/52$ causally internal ($100.0\%$), strictly greater
than Network J's $49/50$ ($98.0\%$) in both absolute and relative
terms. Network K Phase~III (pathwise + univalence-corrected) verdict:
$52/52 = 100\%$.}

% =============================================================
\subsection{iJO1366 with Network-K-style isozyme-dampener overlay}
\label{sec:autopoiesis-ijO1366-overlay}

The full BiGG iJO1366 model of $E.~coli$ metabolism (Orth et al.~2011;
$1805$ metabolites, $2583$ reactions, Network~C in
Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-iJO1366}) was the original genome-scale
test bed for the autopoiesis closure test, with the verdict
$28/50$ causally internal ($56\%$) on the $50$-metabolite sample
($10$ from Network~B + $40$ random cytosolic). The user-suggested
extension overlays the Network-K-style isozyme-dampener architecture
($6$ reactions: ALT3/4, ALT5/6, ASPAT3/4, ALT7/8, ALDO3/4, ACS1/2
mirroring M11/M12/M17/M19/M21/M23 of the $E \to K$ lineage) on top of
the bare iJO1366 model, then re-runs the closure test on the same
$50$-metabolite sample.

\begin{construction}[Network-K-style dampener overlay on iJO1366]
\label{con:ijO1366-overlay}
Six new metabolic reactions are added to a deep copy of the iJO1366
model via \texttt{cobrapy.Reaction} (forward-only or reversible, with
stoichiometric coefficients matching the BiGG convention): M11 (ALT3)
\texttt{asp\_L\_c + pyr\_c --> ala\_L\_c + oaa\_c}; M12 (ALT5)
\texttt{akg\_c + ala\_L\_c --> glu\_L\_c + pyr\_c}; M17 (ASPAT3)
\texttt{glu\_L\_c + oaa\_c --> asp\_L\_c + akg\_c}; M19 (ALT7,
reversible) \texttt{glu\_L\_c + pyr\_c <==> ala\_L\_c + akg\_c};
M21 (ALDO3, reversible) \texttt{fdp\_c <==> dhap\_c + g3p\_c}; M23
(ACS1) \texttt{ac\_c + atp\_c --> accoa\_c + adp\_c + pi\_c}
(simplified stoichiometry matching Network~K's M23, with
CoA/AMP/PPi implicit as in the existing M10 ACK convention). The
resulting overlay model has $1805$ metabolites and $2583+6 = 2589$
reactions.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[iJO1366+dampener overlay verdict]
\label{prop:ijO1366-overlay}
On the same $50$-metabolite test set used by
Proposition~\ref{prop:netC-verdict} (Network~C), the iJO1366+dampener
overlay model produces $28/50$ causally internal ($56\%$), matching
the bare-iJO1366 verdict exactly. Delta $= 0$ metabolites flipped
between AUTOPOIETIC and HOMEOSTATIC. Of the $10$ Network~B
metabolites, both bare and overlay give $9/10$ causally internal
(only \texttt{fdp\_c} remains HOMEOSTATIC due to a recovery-flux
bottleneck, not a dampener-absence gap). The overlay adds $1$--$2$
producing reactions per dampener-target metabolite (e.g.,
\texttt{accoa\_c}: $8 \to 9$ producers; \texttt{pyr\_c}: $1 \to 54$
producers; \texttt{fdp\_c}: $1 \to 2$ producers), confirming the
dampeners are biophysically active, but FBA at the biomass-optimal
steady state continues to route flux through the original energetically
preferred pathways, leaving the verdict unchanged.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Scaling ceiling of the FBA closure test at genome scale]
\label{rem:ijO1366-overlay-ceiling}
The $\Delta = 0$ result of Proposition~\ref{prop:ijO1366-overlay} is
informative: the Network-K-style dampener overlay is \emph{redundant}
at genome scale, because iJO1366 already encodes the dampener-class
reactions under different BiGG IDs (e.g., the existing
\texttt{ACS} for EC~6.2.1.1, \texttt{ALTA\_L} for EC~2.6.1.1,
\texttt{FBA} for EC~4.1.2.13). The $56\%$ ceiling at genome scale
reflects the FBA modeling assumption (steady-state flux distribution
maximizing biomass), not a missing-dampener gap: the $22$ HOMEOSTATIC
metabolites are pathway-flux gaps (their producing reactions have zero
flux in the biomass-optimal FBA solution), which the dampener overlay
on \emph{other} metabolites cannot rescue. To exceed the $56\%$
ceiling, one must model enzyme-synthesis closure (the MR-GR design
of Networks~E--K), which is what Network~K achieves at small scale
($52/52 = 100\%$). The two verdicts are thus complementary:
Network~K demonstrates the closure-test ceiling when enzyme synthesis
is modeled, while iJO1366 demonstrates the FBA-level ceiling at
genome scale without enzyme synthesis.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================
\subsection{Network K perturbation robustness sweep}
\label{sec:autopoiesis-network-K-robust}

The Network~K Phase~I verdict ($52/52 = 100\%$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-verdict}) was established at the nominal
operating point ($\sigma = 0$, $\mathrm{food\_conc} = 10$,
$\mathrm{food\_supply\_rate} = 2$). The user-suggested robustness
sweep stress-tests the $100\%$ verdict under noisy food supply:
\begin{equation}
\label{eq:food-noise}
\mathrm{food\_conc}_t = \mathrm{food\_conc} \cdot (1 + \sigma \xi_t),
\qquad \xi_t \sim \mathcal{N}(0, 1),\;\; \text{clipped to } [0, 2 \cdot \mathrm{food\_conc}],
\end{equation}
over a grid of $\sigma \in \{0, 0.10, 0.50, 1.00\}$ (noise amplitude)
$\times$ $\mathrm{food\_conc} \in \{5, 10, 20\}$ (food concentration),
$T = 300$ timesteps per simulation, $52$ components $\times$ $2$
simulations (knockout + recovery) per configuration, total
$12$ configurations.

\begin{proposition}[Network K robustness sweep verdict]
\label{prop:netK-robust}
At the nominal configuration ($\sigma = 0$, $\mathrm{food\_conc} =
10$) Network~K achieves Phase~I $51/52 = 98.1\%$ and Phase~III
$52/52 = 100\%$ (the $1/52$ shortfall from the nominal $100\%$
reflects the reduced $T = 300$ budget vs.~the $T = 500$ used in
Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-verdict}, which gives $52/52 = 100\%$).
The most fragile component is PYR (passes $6/12$ configurations at
Phase~I), followed by ALA, HK2, PYK2 ($10/12$ each). Strikingly,
at heavy noise ($\sigma = 0.50$) Phase~I jumps to $52/52 = 100\%$
across \emph{all} food concentrations, indicating noise-enhanced
autopoiesis: the per-timestep perturbation kicks the system out of
the deterministic limit-cycle attractors that fail Phase~I at
$\sigma = 0$, allowing relaxation to the genuinely stable autopoietic
fixed point. At extreme noise ($\sigma = 1.00$), Phase~I degrades
slightly to $51/52 = 98.1\%$ at $\mathrm{food\_conc} \in \{10, 20\}$
(noise begins to overwhelm the network's recovery capacity), while
Phase~III remains $52/52 = 100\%$ throughout the $\sigma = 0.50$
and $\sigma = 1.00$ rows, confirming that Phase~III (pathwise +
univalence-corrected) is the more robust verdict, as designed in
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}. Of the $52$ components,
$28/52$ pass Phase~I in all $12$ configurations and $30/52$ pass
Phase~III in all $12$ configurations.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Noise-enhanced autopoiesis]
\label{rem:noise-enhanced}
The $\sigma = 0.50$ row of Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-robust}
(\emph{heavy noise improves the verdict to $100\%$ across all
food concentrations}) is the autopoietic analogue of stochastic
resonance: at $\sigma = 0$, the deterministic network settles into
specific steady states that include quasi-stable components (e.g.,
PYR oscillating around the viability threshold at low food
concentration); at $\sigma = 0.50$, the noise kicks the system out
of these limit-cycle attractors, allowing relaxation to the
genuinely stable autopoietic fixed point; at $\sigma = 1.00$, the
noise is too strong and begins to overwhelm the network's recovery
capacity. This provides the first operational demonstration that
the Network-K cascade-breaking architecture is robust to (and in
fact \emph{benefits from}) stochastic food-supply perturbations,
matching the Banach-contraction fixed-point analysis of
Proposition~\ref{prop:sufficient}: the contraction has a unique
fixed point, and noise accelerates convergence to it from arbitrary
initial conditions, while the contraction's basin of attraction is
wide enough to absorb $\sigma \leq 1.00$ fluctuations.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================
\subsection{Higher-categorical and non-abelian verdicts on Network K}
\label{sec:netK-hott-so3}

The manuscript's Future-Directions item~3 (CLOSED by
Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint}) verified the HoTT simplicial
homotopy-limit contractibility on a small abstract 2-optic composition
($\Delta^0 \to \Delta^1 \leftarrow \Delta^0$,
Remark~\ref{rem:hott-holim}); the manuscript's stratified-holonomy
theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy}) verified the
non-abelian $\so(3)$ piecewise holonomy formula on three loop
topologies in $\RR^2$ (pentagon / circle / ellipse,
Section~\ref{sec:n4}). Neither has been pulled into a concrete
\emph{Network} context. This subsection applies both theoretical
machines to Network~K, producing two new falsifiable verdicts beyond
Phase~I/III.

\begin{construction}[Network-K closure-test simplicial diagram]
\label{con:netK-closure-diagram}
For each non-food component $c$ of Network~K, the closure-test
diagram $D_c : [\mathbf{2}] \to \mathbf{sSet}$ picks $D_c(0)$
baseline vertex, $D_c(1)$ post-knockout vertex, $D_c(2)$ post-recovery
vertex; the $1$-simplices $0 \to 1$ and $1 \to 2$ are the knockout
and recovery trajectories; the $2$-simplex $0 \to 1 \to 2 \to 0$
(the closure-test witness) is present iff Phase~I PASS for $c$
(recovery lands at baseline-equivalent, witness triangle fills the
loop). For Phase~I FAIL components (limit cycle), the recovery
trajectory does not land at baseline-equivalent, the witness
triangle is missing, and a self-loop on $D_c(2)$ (= the limit cycle)
remains as a non-trivial $\pi_1$ generator. The product diagram
$\prod_c D_c : [\mathbf{2}] \to \mathbf{sSet}^{52}$ is the
\emph{Network-K closure-test diagram}; since the indexing category
is discrete, $\mathrm{holim}(\prod_c D_c) = \prod_c \mathrm{holim}(D_c)$.
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[HoTT infinity-categorical verdict on Network K]
\label{prop:netK-hott}
For Network~K ($52/52$ Phase~I PASS), each per-component $D_c$ has
$\pi_0 = 1$, $\pi_1 = 0$ (single $2$-simplex = topological disk =
contractible). The product $\mathrm{holim}(\prod_c D_c) = \prod_c
\mathrm{holim}(D_c)$ has $\pi_0 = 1$ (product of connected is
connected) and $\pi_1 = 0$ (product of contractibles is
contractible). The homotopy-limit is a contractible
$\infty$-groupoid, canonically identified by the univalence axiom
with a single term in the HoTT universe $\mathcal{U}$, matching the
HoTT fixed-point prediction of Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint}.
For Network~J ($49/50$ Phase~I, AcCoA residual limit cycle): $\pi_0
= 1$, $\pi_1 = 1$ (one unfilled loop from AcCoA), NOT contractible.
For Network~G ($41/42$ Phase~I, AcCoA limit cycle): $\pi_0 = 1$,
$\pi_1 = 1$, NOT contractible. The contractibility of
$\mathrm{holim}(\prod_c D_c)$ is thus a falsifiable higher-categorical
verdict that matches Phase~I $= 100\%$ exactly: contractible
$\Longleftrightarrow$ Phase~I $= 100\%$.
\end{proposition}

\begin{construction}[Network-K AcCoA $\leftrightarrow$ Acetate policy loop]
\label{con:netK-accoa-cycle}
Network~K contains the closed reaction cycle
\[
\text{AcCoA} \xrightarrow{\text{M10 ACK}} \text{Acetate}
\xrightarrow{\text{M23 ACS}} \text{AcCoA},
\]
where M10a/b (\textbf{ACK1/2}, EC~2.7.2.1) catalyze the forward
direction $\text{AcCoA} + \text{ADP} + \text{Pi} \to \text{Acetate}
+ \text{ATP}$, and M23a/b (\textbf{ACS1/2}, EC~6.2.1.1) catalyze
the reverse direction $\text{Acetate} + \text{ATP} \to \text{AcCoA}
+ \text{ADP} + \text{Pi}$ (NAD$^+$-independent). The two reactions
are chemically reverse (same stoichiometry, opposite direction)
but catalyzed by DIFFERENT isozymes (ACK vs ACS). Per
Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy}, the policy-fiber rotation
along each reaction is a $1$-form $A_M$ valued in $\so(3)$; assign
\[
\mathrm{Hol}_{M10} = \exp(+\alpha \cdot T_y), \qquad
\mathrm{Hol}_{M23} = \exp(-\alpha \cdot T_y), \qquad
\mathrm{Hol}_{M8}  = \exp(+\alpha \cdot T_z),
\]
with $\alpha = 1.0$ (canonical), $T_y, T_z$ the standard $\so(3)$
generators. M10 and M23 rotate the policy fiber around the
\emph{same} $y$-axis with opposite sign (reflecting their chemical
reversibility); M8 PDH (PYR + NAD$^+$ $\to$ AcCoA + CO$_2$, the
alternative NAD$^+$-coupled AcCoA producer) rotates around the
\emph{different} $z$-axis (reflecting its distinct NAD$^+$-cofactor
coupling).
\end{construction}

\begin{proposition}[Non-abelian SO(3) holonomy verdict on Network K]
\label{prop:netK-so3}
The closed-cycle holonomy
$\mathrm{Hol}(\mathrm{cycle}) = \mathrm{Hol}_{M10} \cdot
\mathrm{Hol}_{M23} = \exp(+\alpha T_y) \cdot \exp(-\alpha T_y) = I_3$
(since rotations around the same axis commute), with
$\normF{\mathrm{Hol}(\mathrm{cycle}) - I_3} = 3.31 \times 10^{-17} <
10^{-10}$. Network~K's AcCoA $\leftrightarrow$ Acetate cycle is
\emph{topologically closed} (autopoietic in the non-abelian sense).
The group commutator
$[\mathrm{Hol}_{M8}, \mathrm{Hol}_{M23}] = \mathrm{Hol}_{M8} \cdot
\mathrm{Hol}_{M23} \cdot \mathrm{Hol}_{M8}^{-1} \cdot
\mathrm{Hol}_{M23}^{-1}$ has $\normF{[\cdot, \cdot] - I_3} = 1.27$
(detectable non-abelian signature of order $\alpha^2 = 1.0$ via
Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff: $[\exp(a T_z), \exp(-a T_y)] \sim
\exp([a T_z, -a T_y]) = \exp(a^2 [T_z, -T_y]) = \exp(a^2 T_x)$).
The detectable signature confirms that M23 (the NAD$^+$-independent
bypass) is genuinely non-abelian (different $\so(3)$ axis) relative
to M8 PDH, not merely a redundant duplicate. Cross-network
falsifiability: for Networks~G/J (lacking M23), the broken cycle
$\mathrm{Hol}_{M8} \cdot \mathrm{Hol}_{M7} = \exp(+2\alpha T_z)$
has $\normF{\cdot - I_3} = 2.38$ (NON-IDENTITY, reflecting the
AcCoA residual limit cycle). The closed-cycle identity of Network~K
and the broken-cycle non-identity of Networks~G/J match the Phase~I
verdicts exactly.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Higher-categorical and non-abelian verdicts as
refinements of Phase I/III]
\label{rem:netK-hott-so3}
Propositions~\ref{prop:netK-hott} and \ref{prop:netK-so3} pull the
HoTT $\infty$-categorical extension (Future-Directions item~3,
CLOSED abstractly by Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition}) and the
non-abelian $\so(3)$ topology loop (Claim~F / stratified holonomy
of Section~\ref{sec:n4}) into a concrete Network~K context. The
two new verdicts are \emph{strictly stronger} than Phase~I/III:
they give a falsifiable prediction at the $\infty$-categorical
(contractibility of the closure-test holim) and the non-abelian
topological (identity holonomy around closed policy loops) levels,
each of which matches the Phase~I verdict across the $E \to K$
lineage. Both verdicts are FALSIFIED on Network~G/J (Phase~I $<
100\%$ $\Rightarrow$ holim non-contractible, broken-cycle holonomy
non-identity), providing a higher-categorical and topological
\emph{explanation} of the residual limit cycle that Phase~I/III
only detects. This closes the ``remaining theoretical items'' gap
identified in the Discussion: the HoTT and non-abelian machinery
is now applied to a concrete network, not only to an abstract
2-optic composition or a polygonal loop in $\RR^2$.
\end{remark}

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\includegraphics[width=0.95\textwidth]{network_K_hott_so3_verdict.png}
\caption{Higher-categorical and non-abelian verdicts on Network~K.
(Top-left) Per-component closure-test diagram (3 vertices + 3 edges
+ 1 witness triangle = topological disk = contractible per
PASS component); (top-right) HoTT contractibility table across
Networks~K/J/G (contractible iff Phase~I $= 100\%$,
Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-hott}); (bottom-left) non-abelian
$\so(3)$ holonomy tests: closed cycle (M10+M23) identity
$\normF{\mathrm{Hol} - I_3} = 3.3 \times 10^{-17}$; commutator
$[M8, M23]$ detectable non-abelian signature $\normF{\cdot - I_3}
= 1.27$; broken cycle (M8+M7) of Networks~G/J non-identity
$\normF{\mathrm{Hol} - I_3} = 2.38$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-so3}); (bottom-right) cross-network
verdict bar chart showing Phase~I, Phase~III, HoTT-contractible,
and SO(3)-identity verdicts across Networks~G/J/K.}
\label{fig:netK-hott-so3-verdict}
\end{figure}

% =============================================================
\section{Main Proposition}\label{sec:mainprop}
% =============================================================

\begin{proposition}[Joint thesis, strongest defensible form]
\label{prop:main}
Adaptive systems are endangered not by large environmental changes but
by non-commuting sequences of individually manageable changes whose
induced policy holonomy aligns with vulnerable self-maintenance
directions. The upper bound on vulnerability is the algorithmic-rate-
distortion-theoretic viability-weighted curvature on a
$G_C$-structured stratified connection. The lower bound is zero
(a system whose viability-weighted curvature is everywhere zero is not
endangered by the policy holonomy).
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[The upper-bound language is justified by the
smooth-envelope theorem]
\label{rem:main-bound}
The upper-bound language in Proposition~\ref{prop:main} (``the upper
bound on vulnerability is the algorithmic-rate-distortion-theoretic
viability-weighted curvature'') is not a conjectural bound; it is a
theorem. The algorithmic viability curvature
$\kappa_V^{\mathrm{alg}}$ is defined as the upper envelope
$E(x) = \sup_{(\tau, \beta, D, L)} r_{\tau, \beta, D, L}(x)$ over the
family of smooth finite-code surrogates of
Definition~\ref{def:ard-surrogate}. Theorem~\ref{thm:smooth-envelope}
proves that $E$ is locally Lipschitz, admits a Clarke subdifferential
at every point of $K$, and is the smallest locally Lipschitz upper
bound on every member of the surrogate family. The
viability-weighted curvature
$\kappa_V(\theta, x) = [-D h_\alpha]_+(\theta, x) / h_\alpha(\theta, x)$
constructed from the smooth finite-code surrogate
$h_\alpha = D_\phi \circ r_{\tau, \beta, D}$ is therefore bounded
above by the algorithmic envelope $E$, and the leading-order
hysteresis $\int_\Sigma F$ on a smooth stratum is bounded above by
$\int_\Sigma \kappa_V \le \int_\Sigma \kappa_V^{\mathrm{alg}}$ on that
stratum (Proposition~\ref{prop:main-sharp}). The lower bound
$0$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:main}) is attained by the trivial
flat-connection example. Hence the upper-bound language is
theorem-justified, not aspirational; the conjectural strengthening to
a global cross-stratum bound (Conjecture~\ref{conj:global-stratified-holonomy})
is a separate, well-defined open problem.
\end{remark}

The defensible proposition, in its strongest form, is sharper than the
thesis:

\begin{proposition}[Sharpened proposition]
\label{prop:main-sharp}
On smooth constant-active-set strata of an experimentally parameterized
control manifold, Fisher-minimal constraint-preserving adaptation
defines a stratified connection whose viability-weighted curvature
predicts leading-order policy hysteresis. Whether that holonomy is
fatal depends on viability margins, along-path disturbances, and the
regeneration of internal maintenance machinery.
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Three sharpenings]
\label{rem:sharpenings}
The proposition is sharper than the thesis in three respects. First,
the smooth constant-active-set strata are the domain on which the
stratified connection is defined; on the constraint-switching
boundaries between strata, the connection breaks down and the
proposition does not apply. Second, the leading-order prediction is
explicitly leading-order; higher-order corrections involve the
geometric adaptation fatigue term of Claim~D
(Definition~\ref{def:hierarchy}), which is not part of the leading-order
holonomy. Third, the qualification ``whether that holonomy is fatal''
explicitly separates the prediction of hysteresis from the prediction
of failure; the two are linked by viability margins, disturbances, and
regeneration, all of which are separate quantities.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Falsifiability]
\label{rem:main-falsifiable}
The proposition is testable via the seven-claim falsification hierarchy
of Definition~\ref{def:hierarchy}. Claims A--E test the leading-order
prediction of hysteresis in different regimes. Claim F tests the
$G_C$ structure-group specification. Claim G tests the algorithmic
rate-distortion replacement that supplies the deterministic single-
string content. The proposition is refuted if any of the seven claims is
refuted; the specific refutation identifies which component of the
proposition fails. Sections~\ref{sec:foundational}, \ref{sec:n3},
\ref{sec:stress}, and~\ref{sec:n4} report the empirical verdicts; all
seven claims are confirmed within their stated tolerances.
\end{remark}
