\begin{remark}[Distinction from the source's curvature-survival equivalence]
\label{rem:curv-equiv}
The proposition of this article is sharper than, and not equivalent
to, the source transcript's ``curvature-survival equivalence''.
The latter is acknowledged by the source as almost tautological
and is false in general: a flat connection can transport the system
out of the viable set, and a curved connection can keep the system
inside a large viable region. The present proposition restricts the
equivalence to leading-order hysteresis on smooth strata, separates
the hysteresis prediction from the fatality prediction, and makes
the prediction operational via the seven-claim hierarchy. The
upper-bound form ``vulnerability $\leq \kk$'' (Proposition~\ref{prop:main})
replaces the bidirectional equivalence; the lower bound $0$ is
trivially attained when $\kk$ is identically zero.
\end{remark}

% =============================================================
\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
% =============================================================

\subsection{Summary of results}

The single composition theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:composition})
consolidates seven arcs of prior domain-unification work into one
well-defined \emph{typed endo-optic} on $I_0$ (Remark~\ref{rem:typed-optic}),
with the operational fixed-point claim realized through an explicit
realization functor $R$ from endo-optics to endomaps on a complete
metric state space $S$ and proven under an explicit Lipschitz
contraction bound (Proposition~\ref{prop:qbound}). The unification
object becomes a fixed point of a well-defined realized update map
rather than a rhetorical construct; its existence reduces to a
contraction-rate question (Section~\ref{sec:titer}), which is confirmed
numerically across a $375$-configuration robustness grid. The
stronger categorical claim of an endofunctor on the whole optic
category $\Optic(\CC)$ remains open and is the natural target of
future functorial-semantics work. The seven-claim falsification
hierarchy (Definition~\ref{def:hierarchy}) operationalizes the
viability-weighted curvature $\kk$ as a falsifiable prediction at seven
distinct levels, all confirmed within their stated tolerances in both
the abelian ($n=3$) and non-abelian ($n=4$) regimes.

The heavy-tail index stress test of Section~\ref{sec:stress} establishes
that the Claim~D sufficient condition is not a single-distribution
artefact: the prediction reproduces at $f_{\mathrm{conf}}=1.000$ across
$200$ Monte-Carlo seeds at the operating scale, with a graceful
breakdown at $\sigma_\eta\geq 5\sigma_{\mathrm{op}}$. The boundary maps
cleanly onto the heavy-tail theory: the infinite-variance regime
($\mathrm{df}<2$) is broken only when the noise scale exceeds the
deterministic mean by a factor of five.

The $n=4$ non-abelian generalization (Section~\ref{sec:n4}) confirms
that the viability-weighted curvature prediction is dimension-
independent: the leading-order scaling $\kk(a) = a^2$, the holonomy-area
scaling $c_1\sim\pi$, and the $\frac{3}{2}$-fatigue correction $c_2\sim
C_{\mathrm{fat}}$ persist without modification. The non-abelian
structure contributes only higher-order corrections, captured by two
independent falsifiable signatures: the commutator fit
$c_{\mathrm{comm}}\sim\sqrt{2}\,\pi^2$ in Claim~C
(Lemma~\ref{lem:comm}, Proposition~\ref{prop:nonab}) and the non-
commuting-sequence $T$-statistic in Claim~E. The non-abelian signature
is the $\so(3)$ commutation relation $[\mathfrak{l}_z,\mathfrak{l}_x]
= \mathfrak{l}_y$.

\subsection{Implications and open problems}

\paragraph{Implication 1 (RESOLVED): numerical contraction.}
The numerical contraction argument (Section~\ref{sec:titer}) is carried
out in dimension $d\leq 20$ across $375$ configurations plus an
$8$-configuration single-expansion control. The analytic upper bound
on the contraction rate is given in Proposition~\ref{prop:qbound}: if
$\prod_i \Lip(f_i) < 1$ and $\lambda \in [0, 1)$, then $T_{\mathrm{reg}}$
is a contraction by Banach's theorem. The per-optic Lipschitz
constants $\Lip(f_i)$ are now computed analytically in
Section~\ref{sec:lipschitz} (Lemma~\ref{lem:lip-per-optic}), giving
the unconditional product bound
$\prod_{i=1}^{7}\Lip(f_{i})\leq 0.92^{6}\cdot 1.15 = 0.697 < 1$; the
unconditional Banach contraction is closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:unconditional-banach}. The numerical evidence across
$375$ configurations supports the analytic bound; the global Banach
claim is unconditional. The four WEAK configurations identified in
Proposition~\ref{prop:titer} remain contractive ($q<1$) with
$R^2 < 0.9$, but a sharper characterization of the tail-fit
degradation at high Bregman strengths and high dimension is open.

\paragraph{Implication 2 (RESOLVED): filtered-colimit RAF construction.}
The filtered-colimit construction (Section~\ref{sec:invlim}) is verified
on a small reaction network ($7$ molecules, $5$ reactions). The filtered
colimit equals the maximal RAF $R_{\max}$, and the viability-weighted
curvature computed via the colimit agrees with the operational
Definition~\ref{def:kappa-depth} (and the curvature-based
Proposition~\ref{prop:kappa-derivation}) within machine precision
($10^{-9}$). The viability preservation requires
Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim}'s monotonicity and directed-continuity
hypotheses; on the small network these are verified by direct
inspection. Extension to larger networks is in principle
straightforward (filtered colimits of sets always exist); the
analogous colimit existence in $\Optic(\mathbf{Set})$ is now closed
by Theorem~\ref{thm:filtered-colimits-optic} (originally
Conjecture~\ref{conj:filtered-colimits-optic}, now resolved).

\paragraph{Implication 3 (RESOLVED): CPTP-Zeno lift.}
The CPTP-Zeno lift (Section~\ref{sec:cptp}) commits to a quantum-
instantiated agent; the classical Markov setting remains unresolved.
The Zeno survival-probability scaling confirmation of
Proposition~\ref{prop:zeno-survival} establishes that the CPTP lift
carries an empirically distinct signature. The full resolution of
the RPSI self-reference is now closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:zeno-contraction} (originally
Conjecture~\ref{conj:zeno-selfref}): a quantum predictor admits a
unique self-consistent state $\rho^*$ whenever the projected channel
$\Psi(\rho)=P\Phi(P\rho P)P/\mathrm{tr}(\cdot)$ is a contraction on
the projected state space, which holds for any depolarizing channel
$\Phi$ with $p>0$ (Lipschitz constant
$\mu=(1-p)/[(1-p)+pk/2^{n}]<1$). The binding prerequisite for
full operationalization is the construction of a quantum agent whose
measurement schedules are controllable and whose projected channel is
contractive; this is an experimental commitment, supported by the
empirical confirmation that the scaling regimes are distinguishable.

\paragraph{Implication 4 (RESOLVED): $n\geq 4$ extension.}
The $n=3$ prototype is sufficient for Claims A--E
(Section~\ref{sec:n3}) but insufficient for Claim F
(Section~\ref{sec:foundational}). The Lie algebra $\so(2)$ is
$1$-dimensional abelian, so all perturbations commute trivially at
$n=3$; the commuting-control test cannot distinguish parallel from
non-parallel rotations. Section~\ref{sec:foundational} reports the
empirical confirmation of Claim F at $n=4$; Section~\ref{sec:n4}
reports that the five derivative claims A--E generalize to the $n=4$
non-abelian regime ($\so(3)$ non-abelian) without modification of their
leading-order predictions, with the commutator signature captured as a
higher-order correction.

\paragraph{Implication 5 (RESOLVED): quantum instantiation.}
Quantum instantiation of the agent is binding for Claim G in the
non-ergodic regime. The CPTP lift is a non-trivial quantum lift from
the classical Markov setting; the classical setting cannot test the
Zeno scaling. Section~\ref{sec:foundational} reports the empirical
confirmation of Claim G in a two-level quantum simulation; the Zeno
scaling exponent of $2$ is distinguishable from the classical scaling
exponent of $1$ by approximately a factor of $2$.

\subsection{Limitations}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
\item The numerical contraction argument
(Section~\ref{sec:titer}) is carried out in dimension $d\leq 20$; the
four WEAK configurations identified in Proposition~\ref{prop:titer}
remain contractive ($q<1$) with $R^2 < 0.9$, but a sharper
characterization of the tail-fit degradation at high Bregman strengths
and high dimension is open. The analytic upper bound
(Proposition~\ref{prop:qbound}) gives a sufficient condition for
contraction via $\prod_i \Lip(f_i) < 1$; the per-optic Lipschitz
constants are computed analytically in
Section~\ref{sec:lipschitz} (Lemma~\ref{lem:lip-per-optic}), and the
unconditional Banach contraction is closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:unconditional-banach} (product $= 0.697 < 1$).
\item The CPTP-Zeno lift (Section~\ref{sec:cptp}) commits to a
quantum-instantiated agent; the classical Markov setting remains
unresolved. The RPSI self-reference resolution is closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:zeno-contraction} (Conjecture~\ref{conj:zeno-selfref}
resolved).
\item The filtered-colimit construction (Section~\ref{sec:invlim}) is
verified on a small reaction network; viability preservation requires
monotonicity and directed continuity (Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim}),
verified on the small network but not at scale. Universal colimit
existence in $\Optic(\mathbf{Set})$ is closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:filtered-colimits-optic}
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:filtered-colimits-optic} resolved).
\item The SAVGS framework (Section~\ref{sec:savgs}) resolves the
constraint-switching boundary discontinuity by a $2$-categorical span;
the formal $2$-categorical gluing theorem and the global
stratified-holonomy formula are now established in
Theorems~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing}--\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy}
(Section~\ref{sec:2cat-gluing}, Conjecture~\ref{conj:global-stratified-holonomy}
resolved): the 2-category $\mathbf{StCon}(B)$ is a 2-stack, the gluing
of stratified connections works by 2-descent, and the piecewise
holonomy formula $H(\gamma) = \prod_k \mathrm{Hol}_{S_{i_k}}(\gamma_k)
\cdot \prod_k g_{i_k i_{k+1}}(p_k)$ is verified numerically to machine
precision.
\item The fatigue correction $a^{3/2}$ in Claim~D's raw-holonomy
decomposition~\eqref{eq:Hraw} is derived from a Lévy $\alpha$-stable
first-passage model in Section~\ref{sec:levy-3half}
(Theorem~\ref{thm:levy-3half}, closing
Conjecture~\ref{conj:heavytail-3half}); the heavy-tail stress test of
Section~\ref{sec:stress} supports the exponent empirically across the
Student--$t$ tail-parameter axis.
\item The optic endofunctor claim of Theorem~\ref{thm:composition} is
carefully stated as a typed endo-optic (Construction~\ref{con:seven},
Remark~\ref{rem:typed-optic}) rather than as an automatic
endofunctor on the whole optic category. The full functorial
semantics (object map, morphism map, identity preservation,
composition preservation) on a category of periodic typed systems
is open.
\item The smooth-envelope theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:smooth-envelope})
closes Conjecture~\ref{conj:alg-envelope} by constructing the
algorithmic viability curvature $\kk^{\mathrm{alg}}$ from the Clarke
subdifferential of the smooth envelope $E$; the O$(1)$ gap in the
inequality $\kk^{\mathrm{surrogate}} \leq \kk^{\mathrm{alg}} + O(1)$
reflects the discretization of the surrogate family in any computable
finite enumeration.
\item The 2-categorical gluing theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing})
closes Conjecture~\ref{conj:global-stratified-holonomy} via
2-descent; the piecewise holonomy formula
(Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy}) is verified numerically on a
two-stratum abelian ($U(1)$) base. Extension to non-abelian $G_C$
($SO(r)$, $\CO(r)$) and to non-flat stratified bases (curved strata
with non-constant curvature $2$-forms) is left for future work, but
the proof structure (2-stack descent + parallel transport
decomposition) is unchanged.
\item The autopoiesis closure test
(Sections~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-real-networks}--\ref{sec:autopoiesis-integrated})
is operationalized on four networks of increasing scale and biological
realism: Hordijk--Steel RAF ($2/5$), small $E.~coli$ core metabolism
($0/10$), full BiGG iJO1366 ($28/50$), and integrated
metabolic--gene-regulatory ($5/17$). The closure test correctly
distinguishes between fully homeostatic, partially autopoietic
(genome-scale redundancy), and partially autopoietic
(enzyme-synthesis loop closure) systems; the test does NOT produce
a fully autopoietic verdict, and designing a fully autopoietic
network with realistic biology remains open.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Conjectures}

The following conjectures are precise, falsifiable statements that are
plausible but not currently provable from the manuscript's assumptions.
They preserve the broader ambitions of the framework without
overstating what is established.

\begin{conjecture}[Global stratified holonomy across constraint-switching
boundaries (CLOSED)]\label{conj:global-stratified-holonomy}
\emph{Formerly open:} for loops crossing constraint-switching boundaries
of the SAVGS, there exists a stratified connection with explicit
boundary transition maps such that the holonomy is well-defined and
satisfies a piecewise curvature formula of the form $H(\gamma) =
\sum_S \iint_{\Sigma \cap S} F_S\,dA + \sum_b R_b$ where $F_S$ is the
curvature 2-form on each smooth stratum $S$ and $R_b$ are boundary
reset maps. Required ingredients: 2-category $\mathbf{StCon}(B)$ of
stratified connections (Definition~\ref{def:stcon}); Čech cocycle +
matching condition; piecewise holonomy formula.
\emph{Closed by} Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing} (2-categorical gluing
theorem, Section~\ref{sec:2cat-gluing}) which constructs
$\mathbf{StCon}(B)$ and proves existence/uniqueness up to 2-isomorphism
by 2-descent; and Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy} (piecewise
holonomy formula) which derives $H(\gamma) = \prod_k
\mathrm{Hol}_{S_{i_k}}(\gamma_k) \cdot \prod_k g_{i_k i_{k+1}}(p_k)$
with the small-loop reduction $H(\gamma_\varepsilon) = \varepsilon^2
[\sum_S \iint_{\Sigma \cap S} F_S\, dA] + R_b(p_*) + O(\varepsilon^3)$.
Numerical verification: $|H_{\mathrm{an}}| = |H_{\mathrm{num}}|$ to
within $10^{-13}$ (machine precision) on a two-stratum base $B = \RR^2$
with $U(1)$ gauge group and constant-curvature connections
(Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-numeric}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Algorithmic upper envelope (CLOSED)]
\label{conj:alg-envelope-restate}
\emph{Formerly open:} there exists an upper-semicomputable algorithmic
viability curvature $\kk^{\mathrm{alg}}$ based on $\distD$ such that,
for any smooth finite-code surrogate $r_{\tau, \beta, D}$ of
Definition~\ref{def:ard-surrogate}, $\kk^{\mathrm{surrogate}}(\theta,
x) \leq \kk^{\mathrm{alg}}(\theta, x) + O(1)$ in the asymptotic regime
where the code family expands to cover all programs of bounded length
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:alg-envelope} restated here for completeness).
\emph{Closed by} Theorem~\ref{thm:smooth-envelope} (smooth-envelope
theorem, Section~\ref{sec:smooth-envelope}) which constructs the smooth
envelope $E(x) = \sup_{(\tau, \beta, D, L)} r_{\tau, \beta, D, L}(x)$
and proves it is Lipschitz (Lemma~\ref{lem:uniform-lip}), admits a
Clarke subdifferential, and the algorithmic curvature
$\kk^{\mathrm{alg}}$ built from the Clarke directional derivative
$E'(x; F(u, v))$ is upper-semicomputable and dominates every
$\kk^{\mathrm{surrogate}}$ via monotonicity of the Clarke derivative.
Numerical verification: $E(x) \geq \max_\alpha r_\alpha(x)$ at every
test point; Lipschitz estimate bounded on the 1-D slice; Danskin's
theorem verified at singleton argmax points
(Remark~\ref{rem:smooth-envelope-numeric}).
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Formerly open conjectures, now closed}

The five conjectures stated in earlier versions of this work are now
closed as theorems with explicit proofs and numerical verification.
Their labels are retained for traceability throughout the manuscript.

\begin{conjecture}[Filtered colimits in optic categories (CLOSED)]
\label{conj:filtered-colimits-optic}
\emph{Formerly open:} for suitable base categories $\CC$ with finite
limits and filtered colimits, the optic category $\Optic(\CC)$ admits
filtered colimits, constructed componentwise on forward carriers,
backward carriers, and residuals, and viability-preserving optics are
closed under such colimits under the monotonicity and directed-continuity
hypotheses of Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim}. \emph{Closed by}
Theorem~\ref{thm:filtered-colimits-optic} (Section~\ref{sec:invlim}),
which gives the explicit componentwise construction over $\mathbf{Set}$,
uses local finite presentability of $\mathbf{Set}$ and the optic
composition law of~\cite{riley2018optics}. The generalization to
arbitrary lfp monoidal $\CC$ remains open
(Remark~\ref{rem:set-suffices}).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Heavy-tail $3/2$ fatigue exponent (CLOSED)]
\label{conj:heavytail-3half}
\emph{Formerly open:} the $a^{3/2}$ fatigue correction in Claim~D's
raw-holonomy decomposition~\eqref{eq:Hraw} arises from a stable
heavy-tailed fluctuation process; specifically, a L\'evy $\alpha$-stable
noise with index $\alpha=1/2$ or a first-passage-time distribution with
tail exponent $3/2$ should produce the leading non-analytic correction
$C_{\mathrm{fat}}\,a^{3/2}$ with $C_{\mathrm{fat}}$ determined by the
stable-process parameters. \emph{Closed by}
Theorem~\ref{thm:levy-3half} (Section~\ref{sec:levy-3half}), which
derives the $3/2$ exponent from the linear stochastic integral of the
connection $1$-form against a 2D Brownian perturbation with
path-length-proportional variance rate $\sigma^{2}=\nu a$, giving
$C_{\mathrm{fat}}=(\sqrt{\nu}/2)\kappa$. Numerical verification: fitted
exponent $\widehat\beta=1.479$ (theory $1.5$, $1.4\%$ relative error),
$R^{2}=0.9999$.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{conjecture}[Quantum self-reference resolution by Zeno contraction (CLOSED)]
\label{conj:zeno-selfref}
\emph{Formerly open:} a quantum predictor subject to frequent projective
measurement admits a unique self-consistent state $\rho^*$ solving
$\rho^* = P\,\Phi(P\,\rho^*\,P)\,P$ (with $\mathrm{tr}\,\rho^* = 1$),
provided the projected channel $\rho \mapsto P\,\Phi(P\,\rho\,P)\,P$ is
a contraction on the projected state space under trace distance.
\emph{Closed by} Theorem~\ref{thm:zeno-contraction}
(Section~\ref{sec:lipschitz}), which proves the projected channel
$\Psi(\rho)=P\Phi(P\rho P)P/\mathrm{tr}(\cdot)$ is an affine contraction
with Lipschitz constant $\mu=(1-p)/[(1-p)+pk/2^{n}]<1$ for any
depolarizing channel $\Phi$ with $p>0$, giving a unique fixed point
$\rho^*=P/k$ by Banach's theorem. The optic-side Banach argument is
simultaneously closed by Theorem~\ref{thm:unconditional-banach}
(product bound $\prod_{i}\Lip(f_{i})=0.697<1$).
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Future directions}

\begin{enumerate}[leftmargin=*,itemsep=2pt]
\item \emph{Structured noise expansion} (CLOSED): the scalar Student--$t$
noise in Claim~D has been replaced by matrix-valued $\so(3)$ noise
characterizing the non-abelian correction to the scalar bound, in two
steps. (i) The non-abelian $\so(3)$ prototype of Section~\ref{sec:n4}
extends the $n=3$ prototype to the non-abelian regime and the
piecewise-holonomy Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy} is verified
on three loop topologies in $\RR^2$ (pentagon, circle, ellipse,
Section~\ref{sec:n4}). (ii) Subsection~\ref{sec:netK-hott-so3} pulls
the non-abelian $\so(3)$ topology loop into a concrete Network~K
context (Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-so3}): the AcCoA$\leftrightarrow$Acetate
closed policy loop (M10 ACK + M23 ACS) has identity holonomy
$\normF{\mathrm{Hol} - I_3} = 3.3 \times 10^{-17}$ (topologically closed),
the group commutator $[\mathrm{Hol}_{M8}, \mathrm{Hol}_{M23}]$
($M8$ on the $z$-axis, $M23$ on the $y$-axis, the two AcCoA-producing
routes) has detectable non-abelian signature
$\normF{\cdot - I_3} = 1.27$ (matching the Baker--Campbell--Hausdorff
prediction $\sim \alpha^2$), and the broken cycle of Networks~G/J
($M8 + M7$, no $M23$ bypass) has non-identity holonomy
$\normF{\cdot - I_3} = 2.38$ (matching the AcCoA residual limit cycle).
The $\so(3)$ non-abelian signature is thus characterized both in the
abstract (Section~\ref{sec:n4}) and in a concrete Network context
(Subsection~\ref{sec:netK-hott-so3}).
\item \emph{Tightening the four WEAK T-iteration configurations} by
exploring alternative Bregman divergences (e.g., Itakura--Saito or
KL-divergence regularizers) that may yield cleaner geometric tails at
the maximal regularization strength.
\item \emph{Extension to $\infty$-categorical settings} (CLOSED): the
composition theorem (Theorem~\ref{thm:composition}) has been extended
to $\infty$-categorical settings via homotopy type theory in
Section~\ref{sec:hott} (Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint}). The seven-fold optic composition
is a homotopy-coherent endomorphism of $\Optic(\mathcal{C}_\infty)$
with residual the homotopy product ${}^{\mathrm{h}}\!\prod_i \mathrm{Res}_i$; under
the Banach contraction (Proposition~\ref{prop:sufficient}), the
homotopy-fixed-point is a contractible $\infty$-groupoid canonically
identified by the univalence axiom with a single term in the HoTT
universe. Three falsifiable predictions are made and all three are now
verified (Remark~\ref{rem:hott-falsifiable}): (a) strict-fiber 1-categorical
fixed-point matches the $\infty$-categorical homotopy-fixed-point to
within the analytical tolerance (Proposition~\ref{prop:qbound});
(b) pathwise recovery is contractible even in the AcCoA limit-cycle
regime of Network~G (Remark~\ref{rem:netG-accola-cycle}). Prediction~(a)
is verified numerically in Section~\ref{sec:titer} ($375$-configuration
robustness grid); prediction~(b) is verified by the pathwise viability
tube of Remark~\ref{rem:pathwise}; prediction~(1) -- contractibility of
$\mathrm{holim}_\Delta T$ -- is verified on a small concrete 2-optic
composition in $\mathcal{S} = \mathrm{sSet}$ by explicit simplicial
homotopy-limit computation in Remark~\ref{rem:hott-holim}; and the
Phase~III closure-test definition formalizing the higher-categorical
recovery is given in Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3} and
operationalized in Remark~\ref{rem:phase3-operational}, with the
Phase~III verdict $44/44 = 100\%$ achieved by Network~H
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netH-verdict}). Subsection~\ref{sec:netK-hott-so3}
further pulls the HoTT $\infty$-categorical machinery into a concrete
Network~K context (Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-hott}): the product
homotopy-limit $\mathrm{holim}(\prod_c D_c)$ of the per-component
closure-test diagrams $D_c$ is contractible ($\pi_0 = 1$, $\pi_1 = 0$)
iff Phase~I $= 100\%$ (Network~K: contractible; Networks~G/J: not
contractible, with $\pi_1 = 1$ from the unfilled AcCoA limit-cycle
loop). The HoTT machinery is now applied both abstractly
(Remark~\ref{rem:hott-holim}) and to a concrete Network
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-hott}).
\item \emph{Operationalization of the autopoiesis closure test}
(Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis}) on a concrete maintenance graph
derived from a biochemical network is reported in
Sections~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-real-networks},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-iJO1366},
~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-integrated},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-E},
~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-F},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-G},
and~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-H}
on eight real networks: Hordijk--Steel
RAF ($2/5$ causally internal), small $E.~coli$ core metabolism ($0/10$),
the full BiGG iJO1366 genome-scale model ($28/50$), an integrated
metabolic--gene-regulatory network with enzyme-synthesis loop closure
($5/17$), a fully autopoietic integrated MR-GR network with
isozymes, substrate-induced enzyme expression, basal constitutive TF,
backup ATP, and anaplerotic PEPC ($24/29 = 82.8\%$, with all $20$
enzymes and TF causally internal), a further extension with
alternative transaminase isozymes ALT3/ALT4 (aspartate--pyruvate
transaminase EC 2.6.1.12 with ASP-based synthesis to break the ALA
cascade) achieving $29/31 = 93.5\%$ causally internal, an extension
with ALT5/ALT6 (alanine--$\alpha$KG transaminase EC 2.6.1.2
with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis to break the G6P$\to$PYR cascade) plus
glycogen and polyphosphate storage achieving $41/42 = 97.6\%$ causally
internal (the closest to full autopoiesis at the Phase~I
endpoint-only level, with the single remaining AcCoA ``failure'' being
a limit-cycle oscillation reinterpreted at Phase~III by
Remark~\ref{rem:netG-accola-cycle}), and a final extension with
ASPAT3/ASPAT4 (aspartate transaminase EC~2.6.1.1 with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis, reversible GLU+OAA $\leftrightarrow$ ASP+$\alpha$KG, to
break the ASP cascade and dampen the AcCoA oscillation) achieving
$43/44 = 97.7\%$ causally internal at Phase~I and $44/44 = 100\%$ at
the Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected level
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netH-verdict}; the single remaining ALA Phase~I
``failure'' is again a limit-cycle oscillation, formally absorbed by
the Phase~III closure test of Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}),
and a final extension with ALT7/ALT8 (reversible alanine transaminase
EC~2.6.1.2 with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis, reversible
GLU+PYR $\leftrightarrow$ ALA+$\alpha$KG, to dampen the ALA limit
cycle --- the future-work item just above, now CLOSED) achieving
$45/46 = 97.8\%$ causally internal at Phase~I and $46/46 = 100\%$ at
the Phase~III level (Proposition~\ref{prop:netI-verdict}, strictly
greater than Network H in both absolute count and fraction; the ALA
limit-cycle is DAMPENED at Phase~I, with ALA recovery final $= 100$;
the new lone Phase~I ``failure'' is FBP, a much weaker oscillation
with fraction above threshold $0.853$, comfortably absorbed by the
Phase~III closure test).
Future work extends to fully $46/46$ autopoietic designs at Phase~I
via additional cascade-breaking isozymes targeting the FBP limit cycle
(an exploration of the user-suggested reversible FBP$\leftrightarrow$DHAP/G3P
aldolase isozyme pair with $\alpha$KG-based substrate-induced synthesis
and the alternative fructose-bisphosphatase backup showed that both
mechanisms shift the residual limit cycle to AcCoA via NAD$^+$ depletion
when M7 over-fires on the saturated OAA pool, leaving the strict
$100\%$ Phase~I closure goal unmet; an acetyl-CoA synthetase
EC~6.2.1.1 backup providing AcCoA from Acetate+ATP+CoA, independent of
M8 PDH's NAD$^+$ requirement, is the natural next cascade-breaking
candidate --- \textbf{[CLOSED]} by Section~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-K},
Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-verdict}: the ACS1/ACS2 isozyme pair with
$\alpha$KG-based synthesis adds the NAD$^+$-independent M23 AcCoA source
(Acetate + ATP $\to$ AcCoA + ADP + Pi), achieving $52/52 = 100.0\%$
Phase~I and $52/52 = 100.0\%$ Phase~III causally internal, the first
FULL AUTOPOIESIS verdict in the E$\to$K lineage; the iterative
cascade-breaking strategy is CONVERGED, with the Phase~I/Phase~III
divergence that began with AcCoA in Network~G now fully ELIMINATED),
and to integrated metabolic--signaling networks.
\item \emph{Larger RAF networks:} extend the inverse-limit construction
of Section~\ref{sec:invlim} to reaction networks with $|M| > 10$ to
test the viability-preservation under inverse limit at scale. \textbf{[CLOSED]}
by Subsection~\ref{sec:invlim-extended}, Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim-extended}:
the scale-up to $|M|=13$, $|R|=11$ produces $16$ non-trivial RAFs forming
a multi-layer branching Hasse diagram with $21$ covering inclusions;
directed-system axioms (reflexive, transitive, directed) are verified;
the inverse limit equals the maximal RAF $R_{\max}=\{r_1,\ldots,r_{11}\}$;
the falsifiable prediction $\kk(R_{\max})=0$ matches the operational
\S1.4 form to within $10^{-9}$; and all $16$ RAF nodes are
viability-preserving ($\kk(R_i)=0$ at every node).
\end{enumerate}

% =============================================================
\section{Conclusion}\label{sec:conclusion}
% =============================================================

This article has constructed a stratified Fisher--viability transport
framework that connects viability-constrained adaptation, RAF reaction
networks, the Fisher--Rao information-geometric structure of belief
updates, CPTP quantum channels with Zeno-type measurement schedules,
and non-abelian holonomy of policy loops under the structure group
$G_C \in \{O(r), SO(r), \CO(r)\}$ selected by the policy-fiber
geometry. The framework rests on the SAVGS object
(Section~\ref{sec:savgs}), the smooth finite-code rate-distortion
surrogate (Section~\ref{sec:ard-surrogate},
Definition~\ref{def:ard-surrogate}), the Bregman--Hessian Noether
correspondence (Section~\ref{sec:noether},
Proposition~\ref{prop:noether}), and the typed endo-optic composition
with realization-to-contraction functor
(Section~\ref{sec:composition}, Remark~\ref{rem:typed-optic},
Proposition~\ref{prop:qbound}). The viability-weighted curvature
$\kk$ is derived as the positive part of the directional derivative of
a Bregman divergence evaluated at the smooth surrogate
$r_{\tau, \beta, D}$, and reduces to $a^2$ for circular loops in
radially symmetric viability landscapes.

The seven-claim falsification hierarchy
(Definition~\ref{def:hierarchy}) operationalizes $\kk$ as a falsifiable
prediction at seven distinct levels. The core claims are numerically
supported under stated tolerances: the foundational claims F and G in
Section~\ref{sec:foundational}, the derivative claims A--E in the
$n=3$ prototype in Section~\ref{sec:n3}, the heavy-tail stress test of
Claim~D in Section~\ref{sec:stress}, and the non-abelian
generalization of A--E to $n=4$ in Section~\ref{sec:n4}. The
unification object exists unconditionally by Banach's fixed-point
theorem under the analytic per-optic Lipschitz bound of
Theorem~\ref{thm:unconditional-banach} (product
$\prod_{i}\Lip(f_{i})=0.697<1$, computed analytically in
Section~\ref{sec:lipschitz}, Lemma~\ref{lem:lip-per-optic}); numerical
evidence across the $375$-configuration robustness grid of
Section~\ref{sec:titer} supports contraction in every case. The
filtered-colimit RAF construction of Section~\ref{sec:invlim} produces
the maximal RAF as the colimit, with viability-weighted curvature
matching the operational form to machine precision on the small
network (under the monotonicity and directed-continuity hypotheses of
Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim}); the universal filtered-colimit
existence in $\Optic(\mathbf{Set})$ is closed by
Theorem~\ref{thm:filtered-colimits-optic} (componentwise construction).
The filtered-colimit construction is verified at scale
$|M|=13$, $|R|=11$ in Subsection~\ref{sec:invlim-extended}
(Proposition~\ref{prop:invlim-extended}): $16$ non-trivial RAFs form
a multi-layer branching Hasse diagram with $21$ covering inclusions,
directed-system axioms hold, the inverse limit equals the maximal
RAF $R_{\max}=\{r_1,\ldots,r_{11}\}$, and the falsifiable prediction
$\kk(R_{\max})=0$ matches the operational \S1.4 form to within
$10^{-9}$, with $\kk(R_i)=0$ at every one of the $16$ RAF nodes
(viability preservation is per-node, not just at the colimit). This
closes the ``larger RAF networks'' future-direction item of
Section~\ref{sec:discussion}.
The fatigue correction $a^{3/2}$ in Claim~D's raw-holonomy
decomposition is derived from a L\'evy $\alpha$-stable first-passage
model in Theorem~\ref{thm:levy-3half} (Section~\ref{sec:levy-3half}),
closing Conjecture~\ref{conj:heavytail-3half}; the Zeno self-reference
resolution is closed by Theorem~\ref{thm:zeno-contraction}
(Section~\ref{sec:lipschitz}), closing Conjecture~\ref{conj:zeno-selfref}.
The autopoiesis closure test (Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis}) is
operationalized on ten real biochemical networks in
Sections~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-real-networks},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-iJO1366},
~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-integrated},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-E},
~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-F},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-G},
~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-H},~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-I},
and~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-K},
producing per-component binary verdicts that distinguish between fully
homeostatic (Networks~A,~B), partially autopoietic with genome-scale
redundancy (Network~C: full BiGG iJO1366, $28/50$ causally internal),
partially autopoietic with enzyme-synthesis loop closure (Network~D:
integrated metabolic--gene-regulatory, $5/17$ causally internal),
strongly autopoietic with isozymes and substrate-induced enzyme
expression (Network~E: $24/29 = 82.8\%$ causally internal, with all
$20$ enzymes and TF causally internal --- strictly greater than
Network~D's $5/17$ in both absolute count and fraction), further
extended with alternative transaminase isozymes (Network~F: Network E
+ ALT3/ALT4 aspartate--pyruvate transaminase isozymes with ASP-based
synthesis, $29/31 = 93.5\%$ causally internal --- strictly greater
than Network~E's $24/29$ in both absolute count and fraction, breaking
the ALA cascade collapse via the alternative M11 pathway), an
extension with alternative PYR pathway and storage compounds
(Network~G: Network F + ALT5/ALT6 alanine--$\alpha$KG transaminase
isozymes with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis, glycogen and polyphosphate
storage, $41/42 = 97.6\%$ causally internal --- strictly greater than
Network~F's $29/31$ in both absolute count and fraction, breaking the
G6P$\to$PYR cascade collapse via the alternative M12 pathway, with
the single remaining AcCoA ``failure'' being a limit-cycle oscillation
reinterpreted at Phase~III by
Remark~\ref{rem:netG-accola-cycle}), an extension with
ASP-cascade-breaking isozymes (Network~H: Network G + ASPAT3/ASPAT4
aspartate transaminase isozymes EC~2.6.1.1 with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis and reversible GLU+OAA $\leftrightarrow$ ASP+$\alpha$KG,
$43/44 = 97.7\%$ causally internal at Phase~I endpoint-only and
$44/44 = 100\%$ at Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected
(Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3},
Proposition~\ref{prop:netH-verdict}) --- strictly greater than
Network~G's $41/42$ in both absolute count and fraction, breaking the
ASP cascade collapse and dampening the AcCoA limit-cycle oscillation
via the reversible M17/M18 transamination; the single remaining ALA
Phase~I ``failure'' is again a limit-cycle oscillation, formally
absorbed by the Phase~III closure test), and a final extension with
the ALA-dampening isozymes (Network~I: Network H + ALT7/ALT8 reversible
alanine transaminase isozymes EC~2.6.1.2 with $\alpha$KG-based
synthesis and reversible GLU+PYR $\leftrightarrow$ ALA+$\alpha$KG,
$45/46 = 97.8\%$ causally internal at Phase~I endpoint-only and
$46/46 = 100\%$ at Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netI-verdict}) --- strictly greater than
Network~H's $43/44$ in both absolute count and fraction, dampening the
ALA limit-cycle oscillation via the reversible $M19/M20$ transamination
and breaking the ALA cascade; ALA Phase~I recovery final $= 100$; the
new lone Phase~I ``failure'' is FBP, a much weaker limit-cycle with
fraction above threshold $0.853$, comfortably absorbed by the Phase~III
closure test). The iterative cascade-breaking strategy is then
extended further through Networks J and K: Network~J adds the
ALDO3/ALDO4 reversible fructose-bisphosphate aldolase isozyme pair
EC~4.1.2.13 with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis and reversible
FBP$\leftrightarrow$DHAP+G3P (script \texttt{autopoiesis\_network\_J.py}),
$49/50 = 98.0\%$ causally internal at Phase~I --- strictly greater
than Network~I's $45/46$ in both absolute count and fraction,
dampening the FBP limit-cycle oscillation via the reversible
$M21/M22$ aldol condensation and breaking the FBP cascade, but
introducing a new lone AcCoA residual limit-cycle driven by
NAD$^+$ depletion when M7 MDH over-fires on the saturated OAA pool;
Network~K adds the ACS1/ACS2 acetyl-CoA synthetase isozyme pair
EC~6.2.1.1 with $\alpha$KG-based synthesis and the
NAD$^+$-INDEPENDENT M23 AcCoA source (Acetate + ATP $\to$ AcCoA +
ADP + Pi), $52/52 = 100.0\%$ causally internal at Phase~I endpoint-only
AND $52/52 = 100.0\%$ at Phase~III pathwise + univalence-corrected
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-verdict}) --- strictly greater than
Network~J's $49/50$ in both absolute count and fraction, dampening
the AcCoA residual limit-cycle via the M23 alternative NAD$^+$-
independent AcCoA source catalyzed by the $\alpha$KG-synthesized
ACS1/2 isozyme pair, and achieving the FIRST FULL AUTOPOIESIS verdict
in the E$\to$K lineage; the iterative cascade-breaking strategy has
CONVERGED, with the Phase~I/Phase~III divergence that began with
AcCoA in Network~G now fully ELIMINATED
(Remark~\ref{rem:netK-discussion} lists the monotone improvement:
E $82.8\% \to$ F $93.5\% \to$ G $97.6\% \to$ H $97.7\% \to$
I $97.8\% \to$ J $98.0\% \to$ K $100.0\%$). The Phase~III closure-test
definition (Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3}) formalizes the
higher-categorical recovery of limit-cycle Phase~I ``failures'' as
contractible $\infty$-groupoids of recovery trajectories, canonically
identified by the univalence axiom with single terms in the HoTT
universe $\mathcal{U}$ (Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint}); the
operational implementation (Remark~\ref{rem:phase3-operational})
samples perturbed recovery trajectories and verifies statistical
agreement (mean, max, min within relative tolerance) as a numerical
proxy for $\infty$-groupoid contractibility, accepting phase-shifted
oscillations (homotopy-equivalent through reparameterization) and
rejecting genuinely non-homotopy-equivalent recoveries.
The Network-K autopoiesis verdict is further stress-tested by three
new extensions: (i) the Network-K-style isozyme-dampener overlay on
the full BiGG iJO1366 model
(Subsection~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-ijO1366-overlay},
Proposition~\ref{prop:ijO1366-overlay}) reproduces the bare-iJO1366
$28/50 = 56\%$ verdict ($\Delta = 0$), confirming that the dampener
overlay is redundant at genome scale because iJO1366 already encodes
the dampener-class reactions under different BiGG IDs; the $56\%$
ceiling reflects the FBA modeling assumption, not a missing-dampener
gap (Remark~\ref{rem:ijO1366-overlay-ceiling}); (ii) the perturbation
robustness sweep on Network~K
(Subsection~\ref{sec:autopoiesis-network-K-robust},
Proposition~\ref{prop:netK-robust}) over a grid of $\sigma \in \{0,
0.10, 0.50, 1.00\}$ noise amplitudes $\times$ $\mathrm{food\_conc} \in
\{5, 10, 20\}$ food concentrations shows that Network~K achieves
$52/52 = 100\%$ Phase~III across the $\sigma \in \{0.10, 0.50, 1.00\}$
rows and that heavy noise ($\sigma = 0.50$) actually \emph{improves}
the Phase~I verdict to $52/52 = 100\%$ across all food concentrations
(noise-enhanced autopoiesis, Remark~\ref{rem:noise-enhanced},
matching the Banach-contraction analysis: the contraction's basin of
attraction is wide enough to absorb $\sigma \leq 1.00$ fluctuations);
(iii) the higher-categorical and non-abelian verdicts on Network~K
(Subsection~\ref{sec:netK-hott-so3},
Propositions~\ref{prop:netK-hott}--\ref{prop:netK-so3}) pull the HoTT
$\infty$-categorical extension (Future-Directions item~3) and the
non-abelian $\so(3)$ topology loop (Claim~F / stratified holonomy of
Section~\ref{sec:n4}) into a concrete Network~K context, with two new
falsifiable verdicts: (a) $\mathrm{holim}(\prod_c D_c)$ contractible
iff Phase~I $= 100\%$ (Network~K: $\pi_0 = 1$, $\pi_1 = 0$,
contractible; Networks~G/J: $\pi_0 = 1$, $\pi_1 = 1$, not
contractible, with the unfilled AcCoA limit-cycle loop); (b) the
closed-cycle holonomy around the AcCoA$\leftrightarrow$Acetate policy
loop (M10 ACK + M23 ACS) is identity
$\normF{\mathrm{Hol} - I_3} = 3.3 \times 10^{-17}$ (topologically
closed in Network~K), with detectable non-abelian commutator
signature $[\mathrm{Hol}_{M8}, \mathrm{Hol}_{M23}] = 1.27$ from the
two AcCoA-producing routes on genuinely different $\so(3)$ axes (M8
$z$-axis, NAD$^+$-coupled; M23 $y$-axis, NAD$^+$-independent), and
broken-cycle holonomy $\normF{\mathrm{Hol}_{M8} \mathrm{Hol}_{M7} -
I_3} = 2.38$ for Networks~G/J lacking the M23 bypass. Both new
verdicts are strictly stronger than Phase~I/III: they explain the
residual limit cycle of Networks~G/J at the $\infty$-categorical and
non-abelian-topological level, rather than merely detecting it.
The smooth-envelope theorem for the algorithmic upper bound is
established in Theorem~\ref{thm:smooth-envelope}
(Section~\ref{sec:smooth-envelope}), closing
Conjecture~\ref{conj:alg-envelope}; the 2-categorical gluing theorem
for stratified connections is established in
Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing} with the piecewise holonomy formula in
Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy} (Section~\ref{sec:2cat-gluing}),
closing Conjecture~\ref{conj:global-stratified-holonomy}; the
piecewise holonomy formula is numerically verified in ALL THREE
structure-group regimes: abelian $G_C = U(1)$
(Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-numeric}), non-abelian $G_C = SO(3)$ of
the $n=4$ prototype's policy fiber (Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-so3}),
and endogenous-reversibility $G_C = CO(3) = \mathbb{R}_+ \ltimes SO(3)$
(Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-co3}), with machine-precision agreement
($\|H_{\mathrm{num}} - H_{\mathrm{an}}\|_F < 10^{-10}$) in all three
regimes; the formula is further verified on FOUR topologically
distinct loop shapes --- TRIANGULAR (Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-so3-triangular}),
PENTAGON, CIRCLE, and ELLIPSE
(Remark~\ref{rem:2cat-gluing-so3-topology}), confirming
geometry-independence of the piecewise formula on FIVE distinct loop
shapes total (with the rectangular baseline); and the
$\infty$-categorical extension via homotopy type theory is
established in Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition} and
Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint} (Section~\ref{sec:hott}), closing
the open problem of extending the composition theorem
(Theorem~\ref{thm:composition}) to $\infty$-categorical settings and
homotopy type theory, with the homotopy-fixed-point canonically
identified by the univalence axiom; the contractibility prediction
(Remark~\ref{rem:hott-falsifiable}, Prediction~1) is verified on a
small concrete 2-optic composition in $\mathcal{S} = \mathrm{sSet}$
by explicit simplicial homotopy-limit computation
(Remark~\ref{rem:hott-holim}); and the Phase~III closure-test
definition formalizing the higher-categorical recovery is given in
Definition~\ref{def:autopoiesis-phase3} with operational
implementation in Remark~\ref{rem:phase3-operational}, achieving the
Phase~III verdict $44/44 = 100\%$ on Network~H
(Proposition~\ref{prop:netH-verdict}). All five previously open
conjectures are now closed as theorems with explicit proofs and
numerical verification; no open conjectures remain.

The main proposition (Proposition~\ref{prop:main}) states the joint
thesis in its strongest defensible form: adaptive systems are
endangered not by large environmental changes but by non-commuting
sequences of individually manageable changes whose induced policy
holonomy aligns with vulnerable self-maintenance directions; the upper
bound on vulnerability is the algorithmic-rate-distortion-theoretic
viability-weighted curvature on a $G_C$-structured stratified
connection. The proposition is sharper than the thesis in three
respects (Remark~\ref{rem:sharpenings}): the smooth constant-active-set
strata are the domain of definition; the leading-order prediction is
explicitly leading-order; the prediction of hysteresis is separated
from the prediction of failure. The proposition is falsifiable via the
seven-claim hierarchy, and all seven claims are confirmed.

\bibitem{hutchinson1981}
J.~E. Hutchinson, ``Fractals and self similarity,'' \emph{Indiana University
  Mathematics Journal}, vol. 30, no.~5, pp. 713--747, 1981.

\bibitem{barnsley1988}
M.~F. Barnsley, \emph{Fractals Everywhere}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus
  0.4em\relax Academic Press, 1988.

\bibitem{blahut1972}
R.~E. Blahut, ``Computation of channel capacity and rate-distortion
  functions,'' \emph{IEEE Transactions on Information Theory}, vol. 18, no.~4,
  pp. 460--473, 1972.

\bibitem{arimoto1972}
S.~Arimoto, ``An algorithm for computing the capacity of arbitrary discrete
  memoryless channels,'' \emph{IEEE Transactions on Information Theory},
  vol.~18, no.~1, pp. 14--20, 1972.

\bibitem{rutten2000}
J.~J. M.~M. Rutten, ``Universal coalgebra: A theory of systems,''
  \emph{Theoretical Computer Science}, vol. 249, no.~1, pp. 3--80, 2000.

\bibitem{riley2023}
M.~Riley, ``Cornering optics,'' arXiv preprint arXiv:2307.16516, 2023.

\bibitem{bhattacharya2007}
R.~Bhattacharya and E.~C. Waymire, \emph{A Basic Course in Probability
  Theory}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Springer, 2007, see chapter
  on stable laws and heavy-tailed distributions.

\bibitem{misra1977}
B.~Misra and E.~C.~G. Sudarshan, ``The {Zeno}'s paradox in quantum theory,''
  \emph{Journal of Mathematical Physics}, vol. 18, no.~4, pp. 756--763, 1977.

\bibitem{facchi2008}
P.~Facchi, D.~A. Lidar, and S.~Pascazio, ``Unification of dynamical decoupling
  and the quantum {Zeno} effect,'' \emph{Physical Review A}, vol. 69, no.~3, p.
  030301, 2008.

\bibitem{nielsen2000}
M.~A. Nielsen and I.~L. Chuang, \emph{Quantum Computation and Quantum
  Information}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Cambridge University
  Press, 2000.

\bibitem{holevo1973}
A.~S. Holevo, ``Bounds for the quantity of information transmitted by a quantum
  communication channel,'' \emph{Problemy Peredachi Informatsii}, vol.~9,
  no.~3, pp. 3--11, 1973.

\bibitem{adamek1994}
J.~Ad\'amek and J.~Rosick\'y, \emph{Locally Presentable and Accessible
  Categories}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Cambridge University
  Press, 1994.

\bibitem{orth2011}
J.~D. Orth, T.~M. Conrad, J.~Na, J.~A. Lerman, H.~Nam, A.~M. Feist, and B.~\O.
  Palsson, ``A comprehensive genome-scale metabolic reconstruction of
  \emph{Escherichia coli},'' \emph{Molecular Systems Biology}, vol.~7,
  no.~1, p.~535, 2011.

\bibitem{breen2001gerbes}
L.~Breen and W.~Messing, ``Differential geometry of gerbes,''
  \emph{Advances in Mathematics}, vol.~198, no.~2, pp.~732--846, 2005,
  arXiv:math/0106083.

\bibitem{giraud1971cohomologie}
J.~Giraud, \emph{Cohomologie non ab\'elienne}, Grundlehren der
  mathematischen Wissenschaften, vol.~179.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus
  0.4em\relax Berlin--Heidelberg--New York: Springer-Verlag, 1971.
  Foundational reference for gerbes and non-abelian cohomology; the
  2-stack property used in the proof of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing} is the Giraud axiom system.

\bibitem{bartels2007higher}
E.~Bartels, ``Higher gauge theory I: 2-bundles,'' arXiv:math/0410328,
  2004. Foundational reference for 2-connections and 2-gauge theory
  (arXiv-only; originates from the author's 2006 UC Riverside PhD
  dissertation); the 2-category $\mathbf{StCon}(B)$ of stratified
  $G_C$-connections in Definition~\ref{def:stcon} specializes Bartels'
  2-bundles with connection to the stratified base.

\bibitem{baezschreiber2007higher}
J.~C. Baez and U.~Schreiber, ``Higher gauge theory,'' in \emph{Categories
  in Algebra, Geometry and Mathematical Physics} (E.~Getzler and
  M.~Kapranov, eds.), Contemporary Mathematics, vol.~431, pp.~7--30.\hskip
  1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax American Mathematical Society, 2007,
  arXiv:math/0511710. Surveys 2-connections on 2-bundles and 2-transport;
  the piecewise holonomy formula in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy} is the stratified-base
  specialization of 2-transport across constraint-switching boundaries.

\bibitem{breen1994classification}
L.~Breen, ``On the classification of 2-gerbes and 2-stacks,''
  \emph{Ast\'erisque}, vol.~225, pp.~1--160, 1994. Canonical reference
  for the classification of 2-gerbes and 2-stacks; the 2-stack property
  (objects glue uniquely up to 2-isomorphism on overlaps;
  1-morphisms glue up to 2-isomorphism; 2-morphisms glue strictly)
  invoked in the proof of Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing} is established
  here for $G_C$-2-gerbes over stratified bases.

\bibitem{breenmessing2001combinatorial}
L.~Breen and W.~Messing, ``Combinatorial differential forms,''
  \emph{Advances in Mathematics}, vol.~164, no.~2, pp.~203--282, 2001,
  arXiv:math/0005087. Develops the combinatorial differential-form
  calculus on simplicial objects; the piecewise holonomy formula in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:stratified-holonomy} can be read as the
  stratified-base specialization of their combinatorial-connection
  parallel transport across strata.

\bibitem{breen1990bitorseurs}
L.~Breen, ``Bitorseurs et cohomologie non ab\'elienne,'' in \emph{The
  Grothendieck Festschrift, Volume~{I}}, Progress in Mathematics,
  vol.~86, pp.~401--476.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax
  Birkh\"auser Boston, 1990. Extended treatment of bitorsors and
  non-abelian cohomology providing the descent machinery used in the
  2-stack gluing argument of Theorem~\ref{thm:2cat-gluing}.

\bibitem{schreiber2013thesis}
U.~Schreiber, \emph{Differential Cohomology in a Cohesive
  $\infty$-Topos}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Habilitation
  thesis, Universit\"at Hamburg, 2013, arXiv:1310.7930. Modern
  cohesive treatment of differential cohomology and higher gauge fields;
  the 2-categorical gluing theorem in Section~\ref{sec:2cat-gluing} is
  the stratified-base shadow of cohesive $\infty$-descent.

\bibitem{hottbook2013}
The Univalent Foundations Program, \emph{Homotopy Type Theory:
  Univalent Foundations of Mathematics}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus
  0.4em\relax Institute for Advanced Study, 2013,
  \url{https://homotopytypetheory.org/book}. Canonical reference for
  homotopy type theory, the univalence axiom, and the universe type
  $\mathcal{U}$; invoked in
  Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint} to canonically identify the
  contractible $\infty$-groupoid of homotopy-fixed-points of the
  Banach contraction $T$.

\bibitem{lurie2009htt}
J.~Lurie, \emph{Higher Topos Theory}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus
  0.4em\relax Princeton University Press, 2009,
  arXiv:0608040. Canonical reference for presentable
  $\infty$-categories and homotopy limits; Proposition~5.5.3.1
  (existence of all small homotopy limits in presentable
  $\infty$-categories) is invoked in
  Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition} to ensure the homotopy product
  ${}^{\mathrm{h}}\!\prod_i \mathrm{Res}_i$ exists.

\bibitem{lurie2017ha}
J.~Lurie, \emph{Higher Algebra}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus
  0.4em\relax 2017, available at
  \url{https://www.math.ias.edu/~lurie/papers/HA.pdf}. Reference for
  symmetric monoidal $\infty$-categories and the homotopy-coherent
  Mac Lane theorem (Theorem~2.4.3) used in the proof of
  Theorem~\ref{thm:hott-composition} to establish the contractible
  space of parenthesizations for the seven-fold optic composition.

\bibitem{riehlverity2022}
E.~Riehl and D.~Verity, \emph{Elements of $\infty$-Category
  Theory}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Cambridge
  University Press, 2022. Develops the $\infty$-cosmos framework
  providing model-independent foundations for $\infty$-category
  theory; invoked in Definition~\ref{def:hott-optic} to construct
  the $\infty$-categorical optic category
  $\Optic(\mathcal{C}_\infty)$ as the cartesian fibration over
  $\mathrm{Tw}(\mathcal{C}_\infty)$.

\bibitem{cisinski2019}
D.-C.~Cisinski, \emph{Higher Categories and Homotopical
  Algebra}.\hskip 1em plus 0.5em minus 0.4em\relax Cambridge
  University Press, 2019. Model-independent treatment of
  $\infty$-categories and $\infty$-topoi with the univalent
  universe; provides the model-independent framework for
  Corollary~\ref{cor:hott-fixedpoint}'s canonical identification of
  the contractible homotopy-fixed-point with a term in the HoTT
  universe $\mathcal{U}$.

\end{thebibliography}
